                              THE ORIGINATOR AND THE SERVICER


1.        THE ORIGINATOR

1.1       Brief Historical Profile

Leasint is part of the Intesa Sanpaolo Group. It has been operative since 1 January 2008 further to the
merger of Sanpaolo Leasint SpA (leasing company of the Sanpaolo Imi banking group) within Intesa
Leasing SpA (leasing company of the Intesa banking group). The company is entirely owned by the
group parent Intesa Sanpaolo SpA.

Leasint is at the top of the domestic leasing sector thanks to a portfolio of about 50,000 clients and 17
billion in deployed funds. With a staff of more than 370 specialists, it avails itself of the co-operation of
about 6,000 authorised bank branches for the sale of products1.

The Company has 38 years of experience in leasing and it is active in all leasing areas, both from a
product point of view (traditional ones and innovative ones such as trademarks, works of art and
energy production plants) as well as in terms of type of client (all areas in the “business” world – from
multinationals to small and medium-sized businesses – as well as artisans, professionals, retailers and
private parties).

Leasint gathers the experience and specialisations of the leasing companies belonging to the banks
that gave rise to the Intesa Sanpaolo Group.

In detail:

Leaseindustria, founded in 1973 under the initiative of Cariplo, with which it shared the distribution
network, the clientele and the same business lines. The company took on the name Intesa Leasing in
1998 within the sphere of the creation of the Intesa Group through the merger of Cariplo and
Ambroveneto.

At the end of 1998 the activities connected with the business operations and real estate area of
Fiscambi Locazioni Finanziarie (leasing company of the Ambroveneto Group) were spun off to Intesa
Leasing. Integration of the Fiscambi Locazioni Finanziarie activities was completed in 2000 by
merging the former companies of the Ambroveneto Group.

In addition, in 1999 Po Leasing, leasing company of Cassa di Risparmio di Parma e Piacenza, was
merged.

At the end of 2002 Banca Intesa BCI transferred its leasing business (formerly Comit Leasing) to Intesa
Leasing.

In December 2007 there was the merger of Sanpaolo Leasint, a leasing company created in 1978
within the banking group of Sanpaolo IMI, into which, in turn, Cardine Leasing, a leasing of the
Cardine Group, was merged in December 2002.

Following the merger of Sanpaolo Leasint, Intesa Leasing changed its name to Leasint SpA.


1
    Figures up-dated as of 31 December 2010.




                                                     59
During 2010, within the sphere of a program for reorganising the leasing business within the Intesa
Sanpaolo Group Leasint took over 58.1% of Centro Leasing, a company in the Banca CR Firenze
subgroup, thus acquiring control of it and creating the Leasing Focus Area within which the two
companies operate with distinctive characteristics and a different distribution network.

Total assets amounted to EUR 18,245.6 million. This represents growth of EUR 374.3 million when
compared to the previous financial year, up 2.1%.

Receivables, generated almost exclusively from financial leasing transactions, amounted to EUR
17,775.4 million (up 2.3% compared to 31 December 2010), representing 97.4% of total assets; the item
Receivables also includes amounts due attributable to transactions involving property under
construction and contracts awaiting maturity for EUR 1,817.2 million (EUR 1,527 million as of 31
December 2010). Net receivables pertaining to impaired positions, as defined by the instructions
issued by the Bank of Italy, amounted to EUR 1,509.8 million (EUR 1,318.5 million as of 31 December

2010) and represent 8.5% of total receivables, compared to 7.6% at 31 December 2010.

The pertinent writedowns amounted to a total of EUR 311.6 million, representing 17.1% of the book
value of the impaired receivables (EUR 1,821.4 million). In the first six months of 2011 gross impaired
receivables were up 13.1% (up 4.1% in the first six months of last year). This occurrence mainly arose
from the alignment of the loan valuations statuses adopted by the group parent for certain shared
positions, causing the changeover from performing receivables to impaired receivables. The
writedowns made on performing receivables, viewed as a standardised category of receivables,
amount to a total of EUR 119.8 million as of 30 June 2011 (EUR 127.6 million in December 2010) and
ensure a 0.73% coverage level for such receivables.

The item Payables, amounting to EUR 16,761.7 million and up EUR 12.1 million (up 0.1%) compared

from Banks and Finance Companies amounted to EUR 16,665.2 million.

compared to 616.4 million as of 31 December 2010.

737.6 million in Reserves (among which a Capital Account Contribution Reserve of EUR 300 million
resulting from the capitalisation carried out by the Group Parent on 22 March 2011 as resolved by the
Group Parent’s Management Board on 8 March 2011) and a negative EUR 17.7 million in Valuation
Reserves basically attributable to the negative Fair Value of cash flow hedging derivatives.

In terms of the income statement, interest income as of 30 June 2011 amounted to EUR 236.4 million,
while interest expense came to EUR 135.4 million.

Net interest went from EUR 99.3 million as of 30 June 2010 to the current EUR 101 million, disclosing
growth of EUR 1.7 million (up 1.7%).

Net interest and other banking income came to EUR 104.7 million, compared to 101.5 million as of 30
June 2010.

Net value adjustments on amounts receivable were up EUR 0.5 million (+1%) compared to June 2010,
rising from EUR 49.6 million to 50.1 million.

Net profit, allocated in full to the Extraordinary Reserve, reflecting taxation at 45.4%, reached EUR
22.8 million, compared to 21.3 million as of 30 June 2010 (up 7.2%).

ROE (calculated as the ratio between net income and the book value of the equity at the end of the
period, net of that income) was 4.9% on an annual basis.

                                              CREDIT, COLLECTION AND RECOVERY POLICY
        1.           LENDING CRITERIA

        The lending process initially involves a review of the application by the approvals department for the
        valuation of the property to be financed, the appropriateness of the price and of the duration of the
        repayment plan.

        Then the matter is reviewed by the Lending Division, which analyses the loan coverage margin, the
        financial and capital structure of the applicant in historical terms, and the prospects in the event the
        transaction is carried out. Particular attention is also paid to the debtor’s business sector.

        Finally, the Lending Division compiles the Supplementary Form, which is also used by the Group’s
        banks for their own purposes.

        1.1           Approval by credit standing ceiling

        The assigning of a “credit standing ceiling” to a customer pertains solely to Leasint; this ceiling
        represents the overall limit for leasing transactions that can be granted by Leasint to a given
        counterparty, whether an Economic Group or individual customer.

        Approval of the “credit standing ceiling” is characterised by the following aspects:

                      –          validity for 12 months after approval;

                      –          category of decision-making authority for “investment grade” counterparties;

                      –          exclusively for activities relating to vehicle and equipment leasing.

        Adhering to the credit standing ceiling assigned by Leasint approval bodies, individual utilisations
        are then approved in a simplified fact-finding process.

        1.2           Leasint independent approval powers

        The following table shows the independent approval powers divided into specific categories
        according to the range of the amount of the transaction, as individually assigned by the Leasint Board
        of Directors depending on the experience and position covered by the various company heads.

        Quarterly reporting to the Board of Directors on the cases approved by authorised personnel is
        mandatory.

           1.3       Decision-making with favourable opinion of the Group Parent

           The “favourable opinion” is the document via which the Group Parent’s central lending divisions
           evaluate and discuss the creditworthiness of positions that exceed defined thresholds for detection.

           A request from Leasint for an opinion from the Group Parent must be made before the pertinent
           decision-making measure is adopted.

           Upon obtaining the opinion, the power to decide on the transactions involved is reserved for the
           appropriate Leasint bodies.

           The independent approval limits as of now, beyond which a favourable opinion must be requested
           from the Group Parent, are as follows:

2.     PAYMENT SYSTEMS AND EARLY DISCHARGE HANDLING

2.1     Payment procedures and systems

By far the most widespread means of instalment collection is RID direct debiting (standing orders for
debiting checking or current accounts by way of collection orders).

RID is a payment method via which the customer, when entering into the contract, authorises the
bank to pay the amounts due under the financial leasing agreement.

For the loading of RID authorisation, Leasint see to the so-called “electronic records alignment”: when
encountering the contract signed by the customer, it is not a bank operator who must take care of
inputting the data into the system but, rather, Leasint, through an “aligning” bank that automatically
issues all the references required into the interbank channel by means of an electronic flow.

Every day Leasint electronically sends the banks involved instructions for the debiting of customer
accounts. Leasint is credited subject to collection.

Currently, Leasint sends out RID flows every day for receivables due up to 30 days after the sending
date. The banks used as paying banks for the credits are Intesa Sanpaolo, Cassa di Risparmio ini
Bologna, Banca dell’Adriatico, Cassa di Risparmio del Veneto and Cassa di Risparmio di Parma e
Piacenza.

For contracts to which the RID process is not applied, which are quite limited in number (for example,
foreign customers, customers subject to Italian Law No. 136/2010 – traceability – non-performing
customers), the collection method is represented by traditional payment systems (bank wire transfers,
cheques, money orders, bills of exchange).

2.2     Change in repayment plans and early discharge handling

Contracts generally do not provide for the possibility of changing the term as set forth in the financing
schedule, nor for early repayment. However, requests from customers are evaluated on a case-by-case
basis, envisaging independent authority for allowing changes to financing schedules and early
discharges granted by Company personnel.

The granting of an early repayment requested by a customer is the responsibility of:

        –    the Head of the Contractual Closures Department for contracts with residual debt less
             than or equal to EUR 250,000.00;

        –    the Head of the Operations Area or Head of Business Management, for contracts with
             residual debt greater than EUR 250,000.00 and up to EUR 1,000,000.00;

        –    the General Manager or a periodically-meeting committee referred to as the
             “Loans/Leasing File Committee” for contracts with residual debt equal to or greater than
             EUR 1,000,000.00.

Requests for calculation of the amount to be paid in the event of early termination of the contract must
be made in writing to the appropriate Contractual Closures department.




                                                       66
The calculation for early repayment is done by applying the discount rate agreed to from time to time.

In any event, the discount amount cannot be greater than the sum of the instalments outstanding and
the final purchase option.

The amount to be paid is communicated in writing to the customer. When acceptance is received from
the customer, which must arrive by the deadline indicated in the mentioned communication in
conjunction with payment, repayment is carried out either directly or by making use of authorised
outsourcing companies.

Requests involving contractual modifications due to events arising after entering into the contract
which involve an increase in the risk, are handled by the Lending Division applying the same rules as
those applicable to new loan proposals.

The formalising of such contractual changes, also concerning the decisions made by the Impaired
Loans Division, is done by the Credit Facility Processing Department.

The Credit Facility Processing Department also handles changes in background data (changes of name
or company name, of legal form, of VAT or taxpayer identification number, etc.).

3.     THE PROCESS FOR HANDLING PROBLEMATIC OR IMPAIRED LOANS

3.1     Detection criteria

The objective of the problematic loan handling process is prompt identification of positions that
display irregularities, and the subsequent consistent management thereof.

For such purposes, the ability to detect (intercept) the presence of possible negative symptoms of
greater or lesser severity and to determine the most appropriate action to take (management process)
becomes fundamental.

With the triggering of specific risk indicators or as a result of indications provided by third parties,
positions are placed in a “handling process” established for the purpose of resolving the irregularities
identified.

The problematic loans management processes at Leasint provide for multiple statuses, each of which
is identified by a given designation and predetermined duration and by specific activities, participants
and managerial/decision-making responsibilities.

The detected positions are placed in the Problematic Loan management processes:

        –     automatically, when signs of risk arise (Instalment Arrears Factor >= 1) as reported by
              the information system;

        –     manually, when the appropriate structures, carrying out their activities, report the
              appropriateness or necessity thereof.

Interception and automatic placement of these positions into the Problematic Loan management
processes occur on a daily basis.




                                                   67
The evaluation of the positions takes place by means of an indicator, the Instalment Arrears Factor3, in
order to promptly identify the first signs of irregularity in positions exposed to credit risk.

Manual insertion, on the other hand, may be carried out upon the occurrence of one or more of the
events indicated as “Negative Symptoms”4, also considering the entity and specific weight of each
one.

Administrators and the respective Heads are required to quickly analyse the causes and, when
deemed appropriate, request their categorisation from the Authorised Decision-Making Body at the
appropriate managerial stage.

In the case of “Detrimental Events”5, administrators and the respective department heads must, on the
other hand, send a proposal to the Appropriate Authorised Body for categorisation as “Watchlisted”
or “Non-Performing”, according to the gravity of the irregularity perceived.

When, on the other hand, it is found that the events that arose are not such as would amount to
impairment of the quality of the loan, they must send a proposal for keeping the account classified as
Performing.

It is also possible for the Auditing Division to request manual insertion – into the “Risk Under
Observation” process or, in the case of the occurrence of detrimental events, under impaired loans – of
positions of higher risk identified within the context of normal activities and characterised by serious
irregularities not detected automatically by the Process given that the causes for such request may not
have involved unpaid instalments or overruns.

Lastly, in the case of technical defaults6, it is envisaged that the Contract Administration Department,
once the collection activities under its own authority (reissuance of the RID) have been carried out
unsuccessfully, carries out manual insertion of the positions into the Problematic Loan process.

In the event of inactivity (lack of manual transfer) as well, or, in any event, upon the passage of 30
days without response to recovery activity by Contract Administration, automatic transfer to the
Problematic Loan process is envisaged

3.2      Problematic loan processes

Following the detection of risk positions, the procedure provides for automatic insertion, according to




3
      The Installment Arrears Factor is defined as the ratio between the sum in arrears and default interest and the
      amount of the most recent installment billed; the threshold for caution is then equal to or greater than parity.
4     Negative Symptoms are defined as events that, assessed individually, do not necessarily indicate a situation
      of difficulty but, due to the specific context or combination of multiple events, indicate the start of a
      degenerative process or the presence of one already prevailing (for example, receipt of first unpaid
      installment, reports from third parties as to negative performance events, etc.).
5     Detrimental Events are defined as events that reflect an advanced process of deterioration or that, in any
      event, reflect a reduction in overall capital coverage (for example, protests on commercial paper or drafts, the
      issuance of injunctions, a significant reduction in capital adequacy, etc.)
6     Technical defaults are defined as defaults deriving from misalignments received from the customer’s bank
      not caused, however, by insufficient funds in the checking or current account for covering the debt.




                                                          68
the exposure7, of the position into the appropriate management process and for identification of the
organisational structure within the Impaired Loans Division to which managerial responsibility for it
is to be assigned.

In any event, the faculty of the Heads of Loan Irregularities to modify the authority structure for
certain positions is not affected.

Two processes for handling Impaired Loans are envisaged:

           –   Ordinary Revolving Process, to which positions are assigned that display exposure above
               EUR 100,000; this process is carried out by the Small Ticket Loan Irregularities
               Department with a limit of 500,000 and the Big Ticket Office for greater amounts;

           –   Simplified Revolving Process, to which positions are assigned that display exposure
               equal to or less than EUR 100,000; this process is carried out by the Small Ticket Loan
               Irregularities Department.

The Ordinary Revolving Process is divided up into the stages of Overdue Item Management and
Specialised Management.

The “Overdue Item Management” stage lasts a total of 60 days. In any event, within the first 10 days
an analysis of the position is performed in order to “confirm” insertion into the Revolving Process or
request eventual categorisation at a higher risk status.

The Loan Irregularities administrator is responsible for preparing a correlated summary outline of the
principal basic queries and, for positions shared with other banks or companies in the Group, contact
with the administrator of the Group bank/company or of the Authorised Loan Department per the
amount range (reports already intercepted in the problematic/impaired loans procedure at the
Banking Group level). Otherwise, in the case of exclusively Leasint positions, the agent who submitted
the report is contacted.

The appropriateness or need to directly contact the customer to request that the contracts be brought
back in line is to be evaluated.

Positions that are not brought back in line upon expiration of this stage are automatically categorised
under “Specialised Management” status.

The “Specialised Management” stage lasts for 30 days. Within that time, the Loan Irregularities
Administrator must prepare a plan of action with the guidelines to be followed in order to clear up the
arrears.

Based on the evidence from the Overdue Item Management phase as well as from any eventual
updates, the fate of the positions is then determined (to keep them in this process or to accelerate to
impaired receivables), triggering further contacts and meetings with the debtor if appropriate.

The Simplified Revolving Process is divided up into the stages of Demands Management and
Collections Management.

The “Demands Management” stage lasts for 60 days. Within the first 10 days a preliminary



7 The exposure is defined as the amount identified by the exposure on Leasint at the Economic Group level.




                                                       69
verification is performed by the authorised Head of Loan Irregularities by amount, in order to
“confirm” inclusion in the Simplified Revolving Process or request a change of process or eventual
categorisation under a higher risk status.

For positions that are kept in the Simplified Revolving process that are not brought back into line
within the first 30 days, the procedure automatically takes steps to send a demand request to Phone
Collection. Only after completion of initial contact with the customer is there an evaluation of the
appropriateness of referring the matter directly to outside collectors.

Phone Collection activities consist of reminding the customer of the contractual commitments,
obtaining a promise to make payment and monitoring the outcome thereof or proceeding to
determine the means for recovering the arrears.

The Loan Irregularities Administrator is obliged to provide support for telephone demand activities
and to follow up on the results.

Positions that are not brought back into line upon expiration of this stage are automatically
categorised under “Collections Management” status.

The “Collections Management” stage lasts for 30 days. During that time, if not yet completed, the
Loans Irregularities administrator selects the collection firm proposed by the system and assigns the
task.

The Loan Irregularities administrator also has the duty to monitor collection activities (the creation of
a summary report on the activities carried out is automatically envisaged).

An “upwards reallocation” is also automatically provided for in the case of positions that, as a result
of increased exposure (e.g. due to the establishment of a new Economic Group, for example), exceed
the threshold envisaged in the Simplified Revolving process. Positions in “Demands Management”
are transferred to “Overdue Items Management”, while those categorised under “Collections
Management” are transferred to “Specialised Management”.

Following completion of the Specialised Management and Collections Management stages, automatic
transfer of the positions to Risk Under Observation is provided for.

The “Risk Under Observation” stage lasts for 60 days. During this phase, the Loan Irregularities
administrator continues to monitor the plan of action/promises obtained from the customer and
evaluates whether it is appropriate to again assign the matter to a collection firm.

Upon completion of this stage, the positions kept in the process are automatically transferred to “Risk
Under Observation for Confirmation”, an administrative status with a duration of 30 days.

During this time span, considering whether or not there has been adherence to the plan of action
agreed to or the promises obtained from the customer, there is an evaluation of the possibility of
keeping the position under Impaired Loans, (i) categorising the counterparty as an “Extended
Observation Risk” or (ii) accelerating transfer to Impaired Loans.

The objective of “Extended Observation Risk” management is to enable the completion of the action
previously agreed to. This stage lasts for 60 days and can be repeated multiple times, upon obtaining
the appropriate authorisation from the Authorised Bodies (Heads of Loan Irregularities departments
and/or the Head of Impaired Loans Division).




                                                   70
Upon completion of the “Risk Under Observation for Confirmation” stage, in the event of inactivity
by the administrator, the procedure automatically categorises the counterparty as Watchlisted.

It is pointed out that, during the phases of Risk Under Observation and the Ordinary Revolving
process, it is still possible to trigger recovery activities by outside firms, if appropriate.

3.3       Impaired loan management process

The definition of impaired loan matches that for “Impaired Assets” used in preparing the “Financial
Statement Format”, as well as that for “Default” as envisaged the New Basel Accord. As determined
by the Bank of Italy, “Impaired Assets” are identified as Watchlisted8, Non-Performing9, Restructured
Exposures10 and Past Due11 positions of more than 90/180 days.

The management of these positions must be carried out pursuant to and in compliance with the
principles and requirements set forth in the New Basel Capital Accord (Basel II) and the rules
subsequently issued by the Bank of Italy, as well as the IAS.

By means of such Impaired Loan management, Leasint and the Intesa Sanpaolo Group intend to
achieve the following objectives:

          –     ensure, at all times, proper assignment of risk status and satisfactory evaluation of the
                loan by prompt categorisation of positions and satisfactory evaluation of the estimated
                realisable value of the loan;

          –     intervene, as quickly and effectively as possible, so as to minimise eventual losses for the
                company by implementing managerial actions aimed, where possible, at overcoming the
                state of difficulty, even by means of useful cooperation with the customer and with the
                other banks or companies in the Intesa Sanpaolo Group.

The Loan Irregularities Administrator is thus required to perform the following:

          –     timely categorisation of a position into the proper risk status based on the detrimental
                events that have determined or could determine a state of default, on the
                reports/determinations coming from other Corporate Structures, on requests for



8 Watchlisted exposures are those with entities in a temporary situation of objective difficulty that forseeably may

      be eliminated within a reasonable period of time. The existence of possible guarantees (whether secured or
      unsecured) applied to cover the exposures is not taken into consideration.

9 Non-Performing exposures are defined as those with an entity in a state of insolvency (even if this has not been

      confirmed by a court of law) or in substantially similar situations, regardless of possible loss forecasts. Thus,
      the existence of possible guarantees (whether secured or unsecured) applied to cover the exposures is not
      taken into consideration.
10 Restructured Exposures are defined as those for which, due to deterioration in the economic and financial

      situation of the debtor, modifications of the original contractual conditions that gave rise to a loss have been
      allowed.
11 Past Due positions are defined as exposures, other than those categorised as Non-Performing, Watchlisted or

      Restructured Exposures, that as of the reporting reference date, have been due or past due for more than
      90/180 days. What is due or past due must be continuous in nature and must exceed the significance
      threshold (5%).




                                                           71
              alignment at a classification with a greater risk status coming from the group parent or
              from other banks or companies of the Group for shared customers, and on the automatic
              reporting resulting from a position remaining in Overdraft/Past Due or Objective
              Watchlist;

         –    timely and effective intervention aimed at limiting Company losses;

         –    constant updating, when provided for, of the appropriateness of the valuation of the
              loan.

(A) “Overdraft” management

The objective of the management of positions in Overdraft status is the elimination of the irregularities
that have given rise to this status, in compliance with prudent credit risk management, or the transfer
to “Watchlisted” or to “Non-Performing”, respectively, in the event that significant improvements do
not occur or there is impairment of the loan.

After assigning the position to one of the two Impaired Loans processes and over time, reaching the
90th or 180th day of overdraft, positions not remedied and not manually accelerated to Default
statuses, if involving real estate property leasing (with at least one instalment overdue) or with an
overdraft threshold12 greater than 5%, are automatically transferred to impaired loans in an
Overdraft/Past Due status.

The existence of an overdraft for 270 consecutive days, with an increase of the threshold to 10%, gives
rise to automatic categorisation as Objective Watchlisted.

(B) “Watchlist” management

The management objective is to overcome a state of crisis with the aim of safeguarding commercial
features so as to contribute to putting the income and capital position back into balance or with the
aim of disengaging from the commercial relationship by recovering the exposure, even if gradually,
characterised by possible strengthening of the appropriate safeguards, by a favourable reduction in
handling times and, obviously, by maximising the percentage of recovery.

For positions that display criticality no longer just at a potential stage but, rather, at an effective stage,
in order to safeguard the overall loan quality and minimise recovery costs a careful evaluation is done
with respect to contractual cancellation (mandatory step for subsequent transfer to Non-Performing).

Thus, whenever Loan Irregularities Administrators deem it appropriate to protect a counterparty loan
under their management by means of taking legal action, they send a contract cancellation request to
the appropriate Authorised Body.

In the case of bankruptcy proceedings, this step becomes mandatory.

Otherwise, for all other positions detected in the problematic loans procedure, the system provides a



12 The overdraft threshold is defined as the highest of the following values:


average of the portions due or past due on the entire exposure determined in a daily basis in the most recent
    quarter;

portion due and/or past due on the entire exposure determined as of the reporting reference date.




                                                         72
daily listing of the contracts for “proposed cancellation”.

In particular, contracts with counterparties that display the following are proposed for cancellation:

        –    6 unpaid instalments if the payments are monthly (3 if bi-monthly, 2 if quarterly or
             otherwise);

        –    4 unpaid instalments if monthly for motor vehicle contracts.

With the insertion of contract cancellation into the procedure, the system prints the pertinent letter to
be sent by registered mail to the counterparty and to any eventual guarantors.

Immediately afterward, the billing of instalments when due is frozen, care of the administrator.

Upon arrival of the advice of receipt or the passage of 30 days from the date of cancellation, the
position is transferred to the Litigation Department.

(C) “Restructured” management

Management activities must ensure verification of complete observance of the commitments
undertaken and prompt protection of the loan, including timely determination of the occurrence ofany
further critical elements or the customer’s success in meeting requirements that demonstrate that the
state of difficulty has been actually remedied and a situation of full solvency has been resumed.

The monitoring, therefore, is carried out both by means of the more general activity of checking the
progress of a relationship (analysis of the quantitative and qualitative economic variables, negative
symptoms, detrimental events, etc.) as well as by means of specific verification with respect to meeting
the management objectives planned.

(D) “Non-Performing” management

The management objective is to recover the loan or minimise losses for the Company.

As a result of early termination of the leasing contract due to breach, after delivery of the property, the
user is required to:

        –    immediately return the property;

        –    pay to the lessor all of the instalments due and unpaid, increased by default interest and
             expenses;

        –    pay an amount equivalent to the instalment for the period after the return and up to the
             time when the lessor actually again obtains availability of the property;

        –    pay, as a penalty, the periodic instalments still and discounted back, as well as the price
             envisaged for the final purchase option, with deduction of the amount the lessor may
             have obtained on the sale of the property;

        –    pay all expenses, fees, taxes, commission and charges incurred by the lessor.

Litigation is to take charge of the position after a decision for the transfer of the customer to Non-
Performing is made by the Head of Impaired Loans.

The matter is transferred from Loan Irregularities to Litigation already completed with the file from




                                                    73
processing and a copy of the early termination letter for the leasing agreement (with the pertinent
advice of receipt form) sent by Loan Irregularities to the customer, to the guarantors and, in copy
form, to the bank where the user is a customer.

Upon receipt of the documentation, the Head of Litigation, Property Recovery and Sales inputs the
status into the information system, which determines the reporting to Non-Performing and the name
of the administrator assigned to the matter.

Before referral to the administrator, the Head reviews the matter, indicating in writing which activities
are to be carried out and the date by which they should be completed in the appropriate “file”.

The administrator of a non-performing case, having received the documentation from the Head,
requests the remaining documentation necessary from records, prepares the legal file and an
analytical outline indicating the action carried out and assigns the matter to outside legal counsel so as
to undertake legal action.

The appointment of the outside legal counsel is granted for the drafting:

        –       of a removal injunction, if movable property;

        –       a summons (or other measure as may be necessary), if real estate property.

The administrator of a non-performing case is in charge of monitoring the turnaround time for cases
taken on by outside counsel, representing the “long arm” of Leasint at court.

The administrator always remains the “director” for all activities carried out by outside counsel:
he/she receives a copy of the court records, verifies their consistency with the action requested and
updates the status on the system along with other possible information relating to the management of
the position.

The administrator also verifies the fees of the legal counsel, so as to ensure that they adhere to the
agreement in force.

After the monitoring activities and service of process, the legal counsel informs the administrator as to
the name of the Process Server empowered to remove the property and the administrator assigns the
matter (by entry into the system) to colleagues in the Property Recovery and Sales Department so that
they may attend to the activities involved in removal of the leased property (contacts with the Process
Server, pickup, property sale, etc.) and promptly checks such work.

Once the property is picked up, the litigation administrator issues approval for sale after verifying the
absence of impeding factors, also providing instructions with regard to the telegram which will be
used by colleagues in the Property Recovery and Sales Department to inform the user and guarantors
as to the deadline for presenting purchase offers of third parties greater than the price agreed to for
sale.

(E) Misappropriation claims

Following failure to return the property and upon prior evaluation of appropriateness, the
administrator for a non-performing case instructs legal counsel specialised in criminal matters to
prepare and file action against those responsible for the offence. Upon filing the action, a request is
made for divestment of the property and, in the case of vehicles with licence plates, a request for the
recording of forfeiture of ownership is made to the motor vehicle registration office (PRA) (through




                                                     74
the appropriate offices).

The claim procedure is constantly monitored by the administrator until the completion of the criminal
proceedings brought.

(F) Insolvency proceedings

Following upon the commencement of insolvency proceedings against the User, the administrator of
the non-performing case assigns the appropriate status based on the type of proceeding (bankruptcy,
prior agreements with creditors’, etc.).

Directly and/or via outside legal counsel, petitions are submitted for the purpose of obtaining
recovery of the property owned and inclusion of the claim via proof of the debt in the proceedings.

With inclusion in the insolvency proceedings (in the absence of differing operational needs), the
administrator issues a request to the appropriate structure for the specified loan to be reported as a
loss.

(G) Report to Management

Pursuant to the powers granted, on a quarterly basis the Litigation Department provides, for periodic
reporting to the Board of Directors, a list of the transactions for which waivers of rights and claims
have been made.

4.     VALUATION, RECOVERY AND SALE OF PROPERTY

4.1    Property valuation

The department tasked with valuing property, within the scope of the Operating Area, is assigned the
activity of valuing the property following the cancellation of a contract due to breach or at the end of a
lease in the event that the User fails to exercise the purchase option and, therefore, is therefore
responsible for the correct valuation of the property and for consistency of the method adopted.

In particular, the department is responsible for the following:

        –    determination of the Estimated Realisable Value of the property (ValpX) initially,
             following prudent criteria, for the purpose of making provisions in the financial
             statements, doing so in adherence to the rules described below;

        –    valuation of the Market Value of the property, utilising outside professionals as well,
             within the sphere of the independent powers granted and in adherence to the rules
             described below.

The department provides adequate quarterly reporting to General Management on the activities
carried out.

(A) Determination of the Estimated Realisable Value (ValpX)

The valuation criteria set forth below are used for determining the estimated realisable value and are
input into the “ValpX System”.

Then, the values thus determined are automatically written down monthly, to a variable extent
depending on the type of property.

(B) Determination of the Market Value of Property

The market value of property must be determined on the basis of commercial sale.

The market value is input into the information system, by the department tasked with the valuation,
in the appropriate Technical Fact Sheet showing the condition and usage status of the property with
any eventual quantification of damage.

Motor vehicles

[Eurotax Blu – 20% (VAT)] – 5.50% (pickup costs) – damages + or – Km, - misc. testing and/or MOTs.

Means of transport

Eurotax Blu – 5.50% (pickup costs) – damages + or – Km, - misc. testing and/or MOTs.

Watercraft

Price estimate emerging from appraisal.

Aircraft




14 The Bluebook manual contains a minimum quote and a maximum quote for the same model of aircraft.




                                                    78
Price estimate emerging from appraisal.

Real estate property

An appraisal, drafted by a qualified expert in the area where the property is located, indicating a
“commercial value” and a “realisable value on immediate sale”.

Equipment

Valuation is done by taking the following elements into account:

        –     usage status and maintenance of the property (theoretical/real);

        –     sale to be made without any warranty;

        –     costs for handling, assembly and possible adaptation to meet safety regulations are borne
              by the buyer;

        –     obsolescence of the property (property still manufactured or not, current sales value new,
              possible availability of technologically superior models, average residual life of the
              property);

        –     commercial viability of the property (specific, standard, prototype);

        –     supplier ratings;

        –     potential number of users;

        –     second-hand market for similar property;

        –     ratio between price goals and sale speed.

4.2     Property recovery

(A) Recovery management

Property recovery requests may derive from the administrators of non-performing cases as well as
from the Loan Irregularities administrators.

The Property Recovery and Sales Department administrator assigned to recover/sell the property
(hereinafter referred to as the “property recovery administrator”), to whom the handling of the matter
is assigned, sends the documentation to the department assigned to value the property in order to
receive the commercial valuation (with the exception of matters involving motor vehicles and real
estate property, for which such request is made by the same department only after
pickup/repossession of the property and the value is to be communicated by the appraisers subject to
on-site inspection).

Upon receiving the technical opinion issued by the department assigned to carry out the valuation, the
property recovery administrator organises removal, usually granting the task to an authorised
outsourcing firm and, in rare cases, on an entirely residual basis, with direct intervention.

Recovery through authorised outsourcing firms

If pickup of the property occurs through authorised outsourcing firms, the property recovery




                                                    79
administrator:

        –    sends the repossessor (outsourcer) the technical documentation for identifying the
             equipment (offer received from the supplier, purchase invoice, copy of the contract, and
             whatever else may be necessary);

        –    requests and evaluates the cost estimate and submits it to the parties with the necessary
             signatory powers in accordance with current legislation and sends the authorisation to
             take delivery of the property with the pickup order.

After removal, the property recovery administrator:

        –    checks the pickup record drafted by the repossessor;

        –    checks whether there are any missing parts and handles appropriate inputting into the
             information system;

        –    examines the photographs provided by the repossessor in order to evaluate the usage
             status of the property;

        –    informs, by email, the Litigation/Loan Irregularities administrator as to the occurrence of
             pickup;

        –    inputs all of the data (pickup data, warehouse name, sq. meters occupied) into the
             information system to update the status of the case.

In the case of equipment that has dimensions and/or importance (with reference to the market value)
that so require, the property recovery administrator carries out an onsite inspection at the customer’s
premises in order to check technical removal problems and possible on-the-spot commercial
advantages.

In the case of real estate property, involving assets with a high commercial value, surrender is directly
monitored, in most cases, by Leasint personnel in attendance and signing the record of surrender
along with the Customer (or the Process Server bankruptcy receiver, etc.).

For this latter activity, the company may make use of authorised service companies so that they
themselves may take charge of the taking of possession of real estate property and managing such
property in terms of ordinary maintenance and, if applicable, extraordinary maintenance as well
(work of a structural type), always subsequent to having examined the work estimates requests and
having consulted our assigned expert.

Direct recovery by Leasint

In the case of direct intervention, the property recovery administrator:

        –    proceeds to check the equipment against the available documentation (offer, invoice,
             catalogues, internet, etc.);

        –    supervises localisation of the property in order to confer the task of recovery to an
             authorised outsourcing firm (even if not interested in the subsequent purchase of the
             property) or transporters and qualified technicians for dismantling the equipment;

        –    requests cost estimates from third parties as per the preceding point for the property




                                                   80
                recovery work;

          –     grants the appointment for recovery using an appointment                              letter   attaching
                documentation enabling identification of the property;

          –     contacts the customer by telephone if the property is being made available voluntarily,
                and the process server, bankruptcy receiver and any other third parties authorised to
                recover the property, to whom he/she sends a pickup order signed by those granted the
                necessary signatory powers in accordance with current legislation.

(B) Property removal

The following events may occur in the removal performance stage:

          –     the property is not found: the property recovery administrator informs the
                Litigation/Loan Irregularities case administrator as to the lack of removal and
                downloads the case from the information system (which reassigns it to Litigation/Loan
                Irregularities);

          –     the property is taken into possession on site and sold on site;

          –     the property is picked up and transported to a warehouse. In this case, the property
                recovery administrator reports the occurrence of removal to the Litigation/Loan
                Irregularities case administrator and inputs the recovery data into the information
                system.

(C) Warehouse management

The property that the authorised outsourcing firms are not prepared to purchase is stored in one of
the Company’s warehouses or at the premises of the outsourcing firms themselves.

Provision is made for periodic visits by the property recovery administrator at the warehouses so as to
check the condition of the property and the surface area occupied.

4.3      Sale of property

The authorised outsourcing firms15, if interested in purchasing the property, may make purchase
offers; if the authorised firm is not interested, on the other hand, market research is carried out in
order to find a potential purchaser.

The guidelines and criteria followed by the Company when carrying out sales activities are described
below:

          –     the sale is made vailing of the Technical Fact Sheet compiled by the department assigned
                with the valuation;

          –     preference is given to sales on site so as to eliminate recovery and storage costs;




15 List of Leasint-authorised firms: Becar srl, Effepi Car srl., G. Ossena & C. sas, Vecar srl, Co.Mar. Servizi srl,

      Interservice Puglia srl, LG&M Macchine srl, Olà srl, S.S.I. srl, Pleiade srl, Immit, RBI srl, Mercator srl, Bistaffa
      Autoveicoli srl.




                                                            81
        –    an effort is made to ensure promptness and speed in the sales so as to limit storage costs,
             reduce the depreciation of the equipment inventory available to a minimum so as to
             enable the case administrator to quantify and make claim for all of the creditor rights of
             the Company and commence action for recovery of the credit vis-à-vis the User and/or
             Guarantors;

        –    disposal of the assets is released from the requirement to obtain multiple offers when the
             sales price is equal to or greater than the market price (with the exception of property
             with a high market value, for which extensive marketing is carried out). In any event, in
             the absence of at least one instance of objective external comparison (for example,
             offerings of the manufacturer or of a merchant in the industry, a telegram to
             lessees/guarantors, etc.), the sale must then be approved by General Management;

        –    a telegram is sent to the lessees and guarantors granting them the power to indicate,
             within 10 days, a possible purchaser who may offer a price higher than that agreed to by
             the property recovery administrator in order to ensure the fairness of the sales price;

        –    the sale of the property is made in compliance with the matters envisaged by current
             safety legislation (currently Italian Legislative Decree No. 81/2008 of the Consolidated
             Safety Act).

(B) Sales criteria for capital equipment

The target sales time is set at a maximum of 12 months for capital equipment.

After determining the market value, carried out by the department tasked with the valuation, and the
subsequent compiling of the Technical Fact Sheet, sales negotiations are commenced with an initial
proposal 30% above the Market Value indicated in the Technical Fact Sheet so as to maximise the
proceeds.

For the purposes of supervising and monitoring sales, the Property Recovery and Sales Department
prepares and keeps a spreadsheet containing the data on the sales that lack an element of objective
external comparison, to be submitted quarterly for signing and approval by General Management.

If a sale cannot be achieved at market value, the property recovery administrator submits the outcome
of the negotiations to the Head or his/her deputy, providing the reasons for lack of sale and
requesting a review of the valuation; if the latter deems it appropriate, he/she requests a review of the
market value from the from the assigned department.

Sales are carried out in accordance with the authority granted.

(C) Criteria for the sale of means of transport

With regard to means of transport, the target time for sales is set at a maximum of 3 months, with the
exception of means of transport sold on site or for which photographic reporting enables an
evaluation of the usage status and respective damage for the purposes of determining market value.

In the case of sales of property already in storage, on-site inspection is envisaged before sale, carried
out by the department assigned to carry out the valuation, along with the resulting compilation of the
Technical Fact Sheet in which the usage status of the property viewed is noted with quantification of
any eventual damage and subsequent determination of the market price.




                                                   82
Sales are carried out in accordance with the authority granted.

(D) Criteria for the sale of real property

The target sales time for real estate property is set at a maximum of 18 months.

After taking possession of the real estate property, an appraisal is carried out by the department
assigned to perform the valuation, availing of an authorised expert in the area where the real estate
property is located, demonstrating the compliance of the property with the urban zoning, the state of
maintenance, the commercial value and a realisable value on immediate sale (market value lower than
commercial value).

The initial negotiations are carried out at the commercial value, directly with potential purchasers or
by conferring a sales mandate to a suitable real estate property agency.

The negotiations may conclude at a value equal to or greater than the realisable value on immediate
sale (market value). If the sale cannot be carried out at the realisable value on immediate sale indicated
in the appraisal, it will still have to be authorised, regardless of the signatory powers, by the
empowered body or by Management.

Sales are carried out in accordance with the authority granted.

Introduction

The Issuer was incorporated in the Republic of Italy on 14 April 2011 as società a responsabilità limitata
under the name of “Zamia SPV S.r.l.” and changed its name in “Adriano Lease Sec. S.r.l.” by an
extraordinary resolution of the meeting of its quotaholders held on 28 November 2011. The Issuer’s
by-laws provides for termination of the same on 31 December 2100. The registered office of the Issuer
is at Via Vittorio Alfieri, 1 - 31015 Conegliano (TV), Italy, the fiscal code and number of enrolment
with the companies register of Treviso is 04454640261. The Issuer is also enrolled in the register of
special purpose vehicles held by Bank of Italy pursuant to the regulation issued by the Bank of Italy
on 29 April 2011. The Issuer has no employees and no subsidiaries. The Issuer’s telephone number is
+39 0438 360 926.

The authorised and issued quota capital of the Issuer is Euro 10,000.00, fully paid up and held by SVM
Securitisation Vehicles Management S.r.l. (“SVM”) (which holds a quota of Euro 9,500.00 equal to
95%) and ISP (which holds a quota of Euro 500.00, equal to 5%).

SVM is an Italian società a responsabilità limitata, whose quota capital is wholly owned by Stichting
Cima, a Dutch foundation (stichting) incorporated under the laws of The Netherlands.

ISP is the holding of the Intesa Sanpaolo Banking Group and, since its shares are listed on Borsa
Italiana Stock Exchange, its major shareholder is Compagnia di Sanpaolo (an Italian Foundation)
holding 9.718% of ISP share capital.

Since the date of its incorporation, the Issuer has not commenced operations other than those
incidental to its incorporation, authorising the issue of the Notes and the entering into the documents
referred to in this Prospectus and matters which are incidental or ancillary to the foregoing.

The Issuer has not declared or paid any dividends or, save as otherwise described in this Prospectus,
incurred any indebtedness.

Issuer’s principal activities

The principal corporate object of the Issuer, as set out in article 3 of its by-laws (statuto), include the
acquisition of monetary receivables for the purposes of securitisation transactions (operazioni di
cartolarizzazione) and the issuance of asset-backed securities.

The Issuer was established as a multi-purpose vehicle and accordingly it may carry out further
securitisation transactions in addition to the Securitisation subject to the provisions set forth in
Condition 5 (Covenants).

Condition 5 (Covenants) provides that, so long as any of the Notes remain outstanding, the Issuer shall
not, unless with the consent of the Representative of the Noteholders and as provided in the
Conditions and the Transaction Documents, incur any other indebtedness for borrowed monies
(except in relation to any further securitisation carried out in accordance with the Transaction
Documents) engage in any activities (other than acquiring and holding the assets on which the Notes
are secured, issuing the Notes and entering into the Transaction Documents to which it is a party or
entering into further permitted securitisations), pay any dividends, repay or otherwise return its quota
capital, have any subsidiaries, employees or premises, consolidate or merge with any other person,
convey or transfer its property or assets to any person (otherwise than as contemplated in the

The Issuer will covenant in the Intercreditor Agreement to observe, inter alia, those restrictions which
are detailed in Condition 5 (Covenants).
